  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 45b, step 35** (hebrewStepName): 'ולא פליגי' reconciles רב זביד and רב פפא; no challenge was raised, so it is not a Resolution. Source: Berakhot 45b ולא פליגי הא דאשכחינהו דקא אמרי נברך; docs/step-labels.md.
  - was: תירוץ
  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 45b, step 1** (hebrewStepName): This finishes the 'שאני התם' answer to the waiter תא שמע, which 45a step 24 labels תירוץ (the source attacks the lenient view, so it is a challenge); the second half takes the first half's label across the amud break. Source: Berakhot 45a–b שאני התם דניחא להו דמקבע להו בחובה מעיקרא; docs/step-labels.md rules 1, 2, 5.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: תירוץ
- **Berakhot 45b, step 1** (title): Title names the step as a rejection of a proof; it is the end of the answer to a challenge. Source: Berakhot 45b; docs/step-labels.md rule 2.
  - was: Finishing the rejection of the waiter proof
  - now: Finishing the answer to the waiter challenge
- **Berakhot 45b, step 1** (stepSummary): Matches the corrected label: the waiter source was a challenge to the lenient view, now answered. Source: Berakhot 45b; docs/step-labels.md rule 1.
  - was: The previous proof from the שמש is pushed aside.
  - now: The challenge from the שמש is answered.
- **Berakhot 45b, step 1** (relationToPreviousStep): Matches the corrected label. Source: docs/step-labels.md rules 2, 5.
  - was: Closes the rejection of the previous proof (from the waiter case)
  - now: Completes the answer to the previous challenge (from the waiter case)
- **Berakhot 45b, step 13** (hebrewStepName): 'תסתיים' closes the proof that רבי יוחנן is the strict one, but it is immediately challenged by רבא בר רב הונא (step 14), so it cannot be a Conclusion in the middle; it is the inference ending the proof. Source: Berakhot 45b תסתיים. אמר ליה רבא בר רב הונא...; docs/step-labels.md rule 5.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: ראיה
- **Berakhot 45b, step 13** (title): Title names the step as a Conclusion; it is the end of a proof that is then challenged. Source: docs/step-labels.md rule 5.
  - was: Conclusion: רבי יוחנן
  - now: Proven: רבי יוחנן

## 202611060248-rev96-berakhot-3

Approved: Drafted overnight Nov 5–6 2026 by Claude from a line-by-line check against the Gemara and Rashi on Sefaria (each finding re-checked before applying); David asked for fixes to go live overnight. Awaiting review by a rav.

- **Berakhot 28b, step 13** (simpleTranslation): Rashi reads וישמחו בי חברי as part of what he prays to avoid: if he stumbles, his friends would gloat and be punished for it ('two evils'). The site makes it a positive request for his friends to be happy with him. Source: Berakhot 28b: ולא אכשל בדבר הלכה וישמחו בי חברי; Rashi s.v. ולא אכשל: וישמחו חברי על כשלוני הרי רעות שתים שיבאו על ידי שאגרום להם שיענשו.
  - was: and that my friends be happy with me.
  - now: so that my friends would not rejoice over my failure.
- **Berakhot 28b, step 13** (simpleTranslation): The closing clause has the same structure as וישמחו בי חברי, which Rashi reads as gloating over a failure that the prayer asks to avoid; reading it as a positive wish contradicts that reading. Source: Berakhot 28b: ולא יכשלו חברי בדבר הלכה ואשמח בהם; compare Rashi s.v. ולא אכשל.
  - was: and may I be happy with them.
  - now: so that I would not rejoice over their failure.
- **Berakhot 28b, step 13** (phrases.7.english): Same error in the phrase row: Rashi reads this as the gloating the prayer asks to avoid, not a positive request. Source: Rashi s.v. ולא אכשל: וישמחו חברי על כשלוני הרי רעות שתים.
  - was: and that my colleagues rejoice in me,
  - now: and that my colleagues not rejoice over me, (That is, gloat over my mistake. רש״י says this would be a second harm, since they would be punished for it.)
- **Berakhot 28b, step 13** (phrases.11.english): Parallel to phrase 7: the prayer asks not to gloat over a friend's mistake. Source: Berakhot 28b: ולא יכשלו חברי בדבר הלכה ואשמח בהם; compare Rashi s.v. ולא אכשל.
  - was: and that I rejoice in them.
  - now: and that I not rejoice over them [over their mistake].
- **Berakhot 28b, step 13** (deeperAnalysis): Same error in the explanation: Rashi reads the 'rejoicing' as gloating over a failure, not shared joy. Source: Rashi s.v. ולא אכשל: הרי רעות שתים שיבאו על ידי שאגרום להם שיענשו.
  - was: It also asks that they all be glad in one another's success.
  - now: It also asks that neither he nor his friends come to gloat over the other's mistake. רש״י says that would be a second harm, since the one who gloats is punished.
- **Berakhot 28b, step 13** (whatToRemember): Same error repeated in the summary line. Source: Rashi s.v. ולא אכשל.
  - was: and for mutual joy among study partners.
  - now: and that study partners not gloat over each other's mistakes.
- **Berakhot 28b, step 13** (mimraCoreRuling): Same error repeated in the core ruling. Source: Rashi s.v. ולא אכשל.
  - was: and for mutual joy.
  - now: and that no one gloat over another's mistake.
- **Berakhot 29a, step 43** (hebrewStepName): This step knocks down the answer of step 42 (יחיד/ציבור); it does not push aside a proof. Refuting a proposed answer is a challenge, as the batch precedent for 'והתניא' knocking out an answer. Source: Berakhot 29a: בצבור מאי טעמא לא ... אי הכי ... מפני ששומע משליח ציבור מיבעי ליה.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 29a, step 43** (title): Title names the kind; keep it consistent with the corrected label קשיא. Source: Berakhot 29a: אי הכי ... מיבעי ליה.
  - was: Rejecting the answer:
  - now: Challenging the answer:
- **Berakhot 29a, step 43** (relationToPreviousStep): Keeps the relation line consistent with the corrected label קשיא. Source: Berakhot 29a.
  - was: Rejects the יחיד/ציבור answer from Step 42.
  - now: Challenges the יחיד/ציבור answer from Step 42.
- **Berakhot 28a, step 22** (hebrewStepName): This is a narrative line (רבי עקיבא tells the Sages to lock the gates). No proof is brought or pushed aside, so 'Rejection' is wrong; the surrounding story lines are labelled מימרא. Source: Berakhot 28a: אמר להו רבי עקיבא לרבנן: טרוקו גלי דלא ליתו עבדי דרבן גמליאל.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 33b, step 11** (whatsHappening): רבי חייא בר אבין raised no difficulty; he is the one who reported 'הלכה', and the question 'הלכה מכלל דפליגי' was raised against his report (by רבי זירא per Rashi, by the גמרא per the site's own step 6), so the site has the roles reversed. Source: Berakhot 33b 'הלכה מכלל דפליגי... אמר ליה לאו הלכה איתמר אלא מטין'; Rashi s.v. ה"ג הלכה מכלל דפליגי: 'והכי פריך א"ל רבי זירא הלכה מכלל דפליגי בתמיה'.
  - was: רבי זירא answers רבי חייא בר אבין's difficulty by correcting the wording that caused it.
  - now: The difficulty about who disagrees is answered by correcting the wording that caused it: the ruling was not stated as הֲלָכָה.
- **Berakhot 33b** (overview): The רבה story is about the bird's nest phrase (רבה was only testing אביי), not about adding praises; only the רבי חנינא story teaches that. Source: Berakhot 33b 'אתה חסת על קן צפור... ורבה נמי לחדודי אביי הוא דבעי'; 'האל הגדול הגבור והנורא והאדיר... סיימתינהו לכולהו שבחי דמרך'.
  - was: Stories about רבה and רבי חנינא teach that we may not add our own praises of God.
  - now: A story about רבה confirms the bird's nest rule, and a story about רבי חנינא teaches that we may not add our own praises of God.
- **Berakhot 35b, step 21** (hebrewStepName): Step 20 was an answer (תירוץ), not a proof; the Gemara's 'ונברך עליה בורא פרי עץ זית?' is an objection to that answer, i.e. a challenge, not a pushing-aside of a proof. Source: Berakhot 35b: אמרי התם משום דלא אפשר... ונברך עליה בורא פרי עץ זית? אלא אמר מר זוטרא.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 37a, step 10** (hebrewStepName): This is the second half of the same תוספתא quoted in step 9 as the challenge 'ורבנן לא?! והתניא'; it is the body of the קשיא against step 8, not a proof for a position (compare steps 4-5, both labelled קשיא). Source: Berakhot 37a: וְרַבָּנַן לָא?! וְהָתַנְיָא ... הַכּוֹסֵס אֶת הָאוֹרֶז ... בּוֹרֵא מִינֵי מְזוֹנוֹת.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 37a, step 25** (deeperAnalysis): Rashi says משמו means in the name of רבן גמליאל, not רבי עקיבא; the site cites Sefaria instead, and its own next amud (37b step 1) says רבי יהודה reports what רבן גמליאל held. Source: Rashi Berakhot 37a s.v. משמו: של ר"ג.
  - was: Following ספריא's reading, "his name" refers to רבי עקיבא.
  - now: According to רש״י, "his name" refers to רבן גמליאל: רבי יהודה reports רבן גמליאל's view in a different version.
- **Berakhot 37a, step 25** (phrases.1.notes): Same attribution error as the deeperAnalysis: Rashi reads משמו as רבן גמליאל. Source: Rashi Berakhot 37a s.v. משמו: של ר"ג.
  - was: According to ספריא, in the name of רבי עקיבא.
  - now: According to רש״י, in the name of רבן גמליאל.
- **Berakhot 36b, step 4** (stepSummary): Per Rashi the caper bud is absent from the detached fruit because it drops off as the fruit approaches ripeness, not because picking removes it. Source: Rashi Berakhot 36b s.v. בתלוש ליתיה: סופו שנופל הימנו כשמגיע להתבשל.
  - was: fall away once the fruit is picked
  - now: fall off as the fruit nears ripening, so they are gone once it is picked
- **Berakhot 36b, step 4** (deeperAnalysis): Same point: Rashi says the bud falls off as the fruit ripens. Source: Rashi Berakhot 36b s.v. בתלוש ליתיה: סופו שנופל הימנו כשמגיע להתבשל.
  - was: come off when it is picked
  - now: fall off as it nears ripening, so they are gone by the time it is picked
- **Berakhot 36b, step 4** (phrases.5.notes): Same point: Rashi says the bud falls off as the fruit ripens. Source: Rashi Berakhot 36b s.v. בתלוש ליתיה: סופו שנופל הימנו כשמגיע להתבשל.
  - was: The covering falls off once the fruit is picked
  - now: The covering falls off as the fruit nears ripening, so it is not there on the picked fruit
- **Berakhot 39a, step 9** (hebrewStepName): 'Both of them did not live out their year' is the last line of the בר קפרא story; no discussion lands here, and the sugya's actual question (מאי לאו בהא קמיפלגי) only starts in the next step. Source: Berakhot 39a: תָּנָא: וּשְׁנֵיהֶם לֹא הוֹצִיאוּ שְׁנָתָן.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 38a, step 15** (hebrewStepName): רב חסדא's 'ולא פליגי' is a statement reconciling two rulings (רב and שמואל); nothing was challenged before it, so it is not a Resolution. Source: Berakhot 38a: אָמַר רַב חִסְדָּא, וְלָא פְּלִיגִי: הָא בְּעָבָה, הָא בְּרַכָּה.
  - was: תירוץ
  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 35b, step 1** (hebrewStepName): "כאן לאחר ברכה" is the second half of רבי לוי's answer, whose first half (end of 35a) is labelled Resolution; rulebook rule 5 (second halves take the first half's label). Source: Berakhot 35a–b: כאן קודם ברכה, כאן לאחר ברכה.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: תירוץ

## 202611060243-rev95-step-labels-1

Approved: Drafted overnight Nov 5–6 2026 by Claude from a line-by-line check against the Gemara and Rashi on Sefaria (each finding re-checked before applying); David asked for fixes to go live overnight. Awaiting review by a rav.

- **Bekhorot 51b, step 1** (hebrewStepName): This line is the second half of the answer begun at the end of 51a (step 28, labelled תירוץ, 'אימא:') to the challenge 'פשיטא, לאו דידיה נינהו'. It completes the resolution; it is not where a sugya lands. Source: Bekhorot 51a-b: פשיטא! לאו דידיה נינהו! אימא: ולא הקדשות בכל אלו.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: תירוץ
- **Bekhorot 53a, step 24** (hebrewStepName): Step 23 is a challenge (אי הכי, בכור נמי לא ליקדש). This step answers that challenge: a firstborn's holiness does not depend on us, it is holy from the womb. Answering a קשיא is a תירוץ, not a rejection of a proof. Source: Bekhorot 53a: בכור בדידן תליא מילתא? ברחם קדוש!.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: תירוץ
- **Bekhorot 53a, step 24** (relationToPreviousStep): Matches the corrected kind (תירוץ). Source: Bekhorot 53a: בכור בדידן תליא מילתא.
  - was: Rejects the challenge
  - now: Answers the challenge
- **Bekhorot 53b, step 24** (hebrewStepName): Step 23 (the ק"ו that new and old should combine) is labelled קשיא, and the identical structure on 53a (steps 4-5) and later on this amud (25/26, 27/28) is labelled קשיא/תירוץ. The תלמוד לומר answers that challenge, so it is a תירוץ; a דחיה pushes aside a proof. Source: Bekhorot 53b: תלמוד לומר: עשר תעשר, בשתי מעשרות הכתוב מדבר.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: תירוץ
- **Bekhorot 53b, step 24** (relationToPreviousStep): Matches the corrected kind (תירוץ). Source: Bekhorot 53b: תלמוד לומר עשר תעשר.
  - was: Rejects the קל וחומר
  - now: Answers the קל וחומר
- **Bekhorot 54b, step 12** (hebrewStepName): A מסקנא in the middle of the sugya. This is the last line of רבא's answer (begun in step 7, a תירוץ) explaining why דגן cannot mean 'all grain is one': the word is needed to exclude other species. The discussion continues straight after with רב הונא בריה דרב נחמן's challenge. Source: Bekhorot 54b: אבל הכא, מי סגיא דלא כתיב דגן? למעוטי שאר מינין.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: תירוץ
- **Bekhorot 57b, step 1** (hebrewStepName): There is no question here. This continues the answer (begun at the end of 57a, 'לעולם גמר') to the challenge that the תנא קמא seems inconsistent. It resolves a קשיא. Source: Bekhorot 57a-b: וּבְיוֹצֵא דּוֹפֶן — סָבַר לַהּ כְּרַבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן.
  - was: תשובה
  - now: תירוץ
- **Bekhorot 57b, step 2** (hebrewStepName): Same answer to the same challenge, continued for מחוסר זמן and יתום. No question precedes it. Source: Bekhorot 57b: בִּמְחוּסַּר זְמַן סָבַר לַהּ כְּרַבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן בֶּן יְהוּדָה.
  - was: תשובה
  - now: תירוץ
- **Bekhorot 60a, step 17** (hebrewStepName): The גמרא quotes the משנה to open a challenge (the next step: it fits רב מארי, but against רב כהנא it is difficult). It does not support anyone's position. Source: Bekhorot 60a: תְּנַן: יָצְאוּ שְׁנַיִם כְּאַחַת ... בִּשְׁלָמָא.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Bekhorot 58a, step 29** (hebrewStepName): 'וכי תימא ... והתניא' is part of the challenge of step 28: it blocks a possible defense so the objection stands. It is not a proof for a position. Source: Bekhorot 58a: וְכִי תֵּימָא: לָא מָצֵי קָאֵי אַטַּעְמַיְיהוּ, וְהָתַנְיָא.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Bekhorot 61a, step 5** (hebrewStepName): Step 4 is a suggested answer ('וכי תימא'), not a proof. 'והתניא' knocks out that answer and renews the challenge to the claim that רבי יהודה holds the eleventh dies. That is a challenge, not a rejection of a proof. Source: Bekhorot 61a: וְהָתַנְיָא: אֵין בֵּין אַחַד עָשָׂר לִשְׁלָמִים.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Bekhorot 56a, step 16** (hebrewStepName): רבי אלעזר is answering an איתיביה (a challenge, step 15) by saying the ברייתא is not authoritative. That answers a קשיא; the next step, his second answer, is already labelled תירוץ. Source: Bekhorot 56a: אֲמַר לֵיהּ: זוֹ אֵינָהּ מִשְׁנָה.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: תירוץ
- **Bekhorot 59b, step 5** (hebrewStepName): This is the second half of the 'אי נמי' sentence of step 4, which rejects the קל וחומר. It finishes the rejection; it is not a separate conclusion. Source: Bekhorot 59b: תְּשִׁיעִי, דְּאַכַּתִּי לָא אִיבָּרַר לֵיהּ עֲשִׂירִי.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: דחיה
- **Bekhorot 57a, step 3** (hebrewStepName): The second half of a צריכותא (why both rulings of רבי יוחנן are needed), matching step 2, which is labelled מימרא. The discussion goes straight on with מיתיבי, so this is not where the sugya lands. Source: Bekhorot 57a: וְאִי אַשְׁמְעִינַן שָׂדֶה — לְחוּמְרָא ... צְרִיכָא.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: מימרא
- **Bekhorot 59a, step 5** (hebrewStepName): 'אילימא מהא דתניא' is the first attempted proof of רבא's rule. It is rejected with 'ודלמא' (step 6, דחיה). The parallel attempts at steps 7, 9, 11 (and 59b step 8) are labelled ראיה; this one should match. Source: Bekhorot 59a: אִילֵּימָא מֵהָא דְּתַנְיָא.
  - was: תשובה
  - now: ראיה
- **Arakhin 4a, step 9** (hebrewStepName): This is the קא משמע לן that completes the answer to the פשיטא challenge (steps 7-8), not the conclusion of a sugya; the parallel קא משמע לן steps on 3b and 4b are labelled תירוץ. Source: Arakhin 4a, קא משמע לן, ואכלת ושבעת אמר רחמנא.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: תירוץ
- **Arakhin 4a, step 13** (hebrewStepName): Same pattern: the סלקא דעתך אמינא / קא משמע לן that finishes the answer (step 12) to the פשיטא challenge of step 11; the series continues right after. Source: Arakhin 4a, קא משמע לן: נהי דזר בהדי כהן לא מצי אכיל.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: תירוץ
- **Arakhin 4b, step 45** (hebrewStepName): Abaye's והתניא is the substance of his objection to Rava (step 44), not a proof supporting a position; the challenge is then drawn out in step 48. Source: Arakhin 4b, והתניא: ראש עבד זה הקדש.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Arakhin 4b, step 45** (title): Title calls the והתניא objection a proof. Source: Arakhin 4b, והתניא.
  - was: אביי's proof
  - now: אביי's challenge
- **Arakhin 4b, step 46** (hebrewStepName): Continuation of the same ברייתא Abaye quotes as his objection (step 45); keep it with the challenge. Source: Arakhin 4b, ראש פרה מכור לך.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Arakhin 6b, step 10** (hebrewStepName): Explains the reasoning of each side of the story in step 9 (one thought it uncommon, the other that it might happen); it reaches no conclusion. Source: Arakhin 6b, מאן דשנייה סבר דלא שכיח, ומאן דאיקפד סבר.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: מימרא
- **Arakhin 10a, step 1** (hebrewStepName): This finishes רבי שמעון בר רבי's explanation that began on 9b step 20, which is labelled תירוץ; the two halves of one statement should carry the same label. Source: Arakhin 10a, ועיבור שנה שלשים ואשתקד עשינו שניהם מלאין.
  - was: תשובה
  - now: תירוץ
- **Berakhot 2a, step 7** (hebrewStepName): This is the rest of the Mishnah's own rule ('and not only this did they say...'): it lists more cases where the time runs until dawn. It extends the rule; no source is brought to support a position. Source: Berakhot 2a, Mishnah: הקטר חלבים ואברים מצותן עד שיעלה עמוד השחר.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 2a, step 7** (stepSummary): Matches the relabel: the examples are part of the rule, not a proof of it. Source: Berakhot 2a, Mishnah: הקטר חלבים ואברים.
  - was: backs up his general rule with two examples
  - now: extends his general rule with two examples
- **Berakhot 2a, step 7** (whatsHappening): Matches the relabel: the examples are part of the rule, not a proof of it. Source: Berakhot 2a, Mishnah: הקטר חלבים ואברים.
  - was: giving examples to prove his rule from Step 6
  - now: giving more cases that follow his rule from Step 6
- **Berakhot 2b, step 14** (hebrewStepName): This 'so we learn from it' is only a first inference, and the very next step rejects it ('No...'). The discussion does not land here; it is an inference that the next step's דחיה pushes aside. Source: Berakhot 2b: אלא, שמע מינה עני שעורא לחוד וכהן שעורא לחוד ... לא, עני וכהן חד שעורא הוא.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: ראיה
- **Berakhot 4a, step 5** (hebrewStepName): Just the last item in the word-by-word reading of the verse (after the advisers comes Yoav). It is part of the statement, not where a discussion lands. Source: Berakhot 4a: ואחר כך שר צבא למלך יואב.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 8b, step 12** (hebrewStepName): No question is asked before it. The Gemara simply gives two explanations of Rava's advice ('some say ... some say'). Source: Berakhot 8b: איכא דאמרי משום סכנה, ואיכא דאמרי משום קלקול סעודה.
  - was: תשובה
  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 8b, step 14** (hebrewStepName): No question is asked before it. The Gemara explains Rava's rule by bringing three readings ('some say ...'). Same pattern as step 12. Source: Berakhot 8b: איכא דאמרי לא תגנו בלא קריאת שמע.
  - was: תשובה
  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 14b, step 28** (hebrewStepName): This step blocks a possible defence of Rav ('maybe it was before the time for Shema') by asking 'then what was Rav Chiya bar Ashi testifying to?'. It is part of the challenge against Rav and is answered by the next step (a תירוץ). No proof is being pushed aside. Step 8 on this same amud has the identical structure (וכי תימא ... והאמר) and is labelled קשיא, so this one should match. Source: Berakhot 14b, וְכִי תֵּימָא: בִּדְלָא מְטָא זְמַן קְרִיאַת שְׁמַע, אִם כֵּן מַאי אַסְהַדְתֵּיהּ.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 10a, step 8** (hebrewStepName): The מין challenges the verse ('should she sing because she did not give birth?'). Beruriah's 'look at the end of the verse' is the start of her answer to that challenge, finished in step 9 (already a תירוץ). A rejection is for a proof, and the מין brought no proof here. Source: Berakhot 10a, אָמְרָה לֵיהּ: שַׁטְיָא, שְׁפֵיל לְסֵיפֵיהּ דִּקְרָא.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: תירוץ
- **Berakhot 12a, step 8** (hebrewStepName): This is the second of two parallel reports (Rabbah bar bar Chana in Sura, then Ameimar in Nehardea) that the Ten Commandments reading was already cancelled. It is a report in the middle of the amud, not where a discussion lands; the parallel step 7 is a מימרא. Source: Berakhot 12a, אֲמֵימַר סְבַר לְמִקְבְּעִינְהוּ בִּנְהַרְדְּעָא, אֲמַר לֵיהּ רַב אַשִׁי: כְּבָר בִּטְּלוּם.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 9a, step 4** (hebrewStepName): Rav Yitzchak bar Yosef reports that R. Yehoshua ben Levi's ruling was only inferred, not said outright. This is a statement about how the ruling was transmitted. It does not push aside any proof (step 2 is a plain ruling, not a proof). Source: Berakhot 9a, לָאו בְּפֵירוּשׁ אִיתְּמַר, אֶלָּא מִכְּלָלָא אִיתְּמַר.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 11b, step 28** (hebrewStepName): Same formula as Berakhot 9a step 4: Rav Yitzchak bar Yosef reports that R. Zerika's teaching was only inferred. It is a statement about transmission; the step before it (Reish Lakish: יוצר אור) is an answer, not a proof being rejected. Source: Berakhot 11b, הָא דְּרַבִּי זְרִיקָא לָאו בְּפֵירוּשׁ אִתְּמַר אֶלָּא מִכְּלָלָא אִתְּמַר.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 16a, step 6** (hebrewStepName): The rabbis' reaction ('had we come only to hear this, it would be enough') ends a story; it is not where any discussion lands. A new משנה follows. Source: Berakhot 16a: אִלּוּ לֹא בָּאנוּ אֶלָּא לִשְׁמוֹעַ דָּבָר זֶה דַּיֵּינוּ.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 17b, step 11** (hebrewStepName): The משנה about working on תשעה באב is quoted as the source of the contradiction (the 'והא איפכא שמעינן להו' of step 10), not as support for anyone's position. Source: Berakhot 17b: והא איפכא שמעינן להו! דתנן: מקום שנהגו לעשות מלאכה בתשעה באב.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 19a, step 28** (hebrewStepName): The ברייתא about burning רבי אליעזר's טהרות and 'blessing' him finishes the challenge of step 25 (that a נידוי is found in the משנה's case). It supports no position; step 29 answers it. Source: Berakhot 19a: ותניא: אותו היום הביאו כל טהרות שטיהר רבי אליעזר ושרפום לפניו, ולבסוף ברכוהו.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 19b, step 15** (hebrewStepName): Like steps 8 and 11 (each a תא שמע ending 'אמאי? לימא אין חכמה...' and labelled קשיא), this תא שמע brings a source to challenge רב's rule of 'אין חכמה'. Step 17 states the challenge and step 18 answers it. Source: Berakhot 19b: תא שמע: ״והתעלמת מהם״ — פעמים שאתה מתעלם מהם.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 19b, step 16** (hebrewStepName): This continues the same challenging ברייתא as step 15 (the זקן ignoring a lost object for his dignity). It is the case that step 17 asks about. Source: Berakhot 19b: או היה זקן ואינה לפי כבודו ... לכך נאמר ״והתעלמת״.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 19b, step 21** (hebrewStepName): A new תא שמע that challenges רב's rule of 'אין חכמה' (a מת מצוה overrides the נזיר's prohibition). The 'אמאי לימא אין חכמה' and its answer come on 20a. It is the same pattern as steps 8 and 11, which are labelled קשיא. Source: Berakhot 19b-20a: תא שמע: ״ולאחתו״ מה תלמוד לומר ... אמאי, לימא אין חכמה.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 19b, step 21** (title): Matches the corrected label: the source is brought as a challenge. Source: Berakhot 19b: תא שמע: ״ולאחתו״.
  - was: New proof: the נזיר and 'his sister'
  - now: New challenge: the נזיר and 'his sister'
- **Berakhot 19b, step 21** (relationToPreviousStep): Matches the corrected labels: both sources challenge רב's rule; neither proves anything. Source: Berakhot 19b: איסורא מממונא לא ילפינן. תא שמע: ״ולאחתו״.
  - was: After the lost-object proof was limited to money laws in Step 20, the גמרא opens a fresh proof that deals with a real איסור.
  - now: After the lost-object challenge was limited to money laws in Step 20, the גמרא opens a fresh challenge that deals with a real איסור.
- **Berakhot 19b, step 22** (hebrewStepName): This continues the challenging ברייתא of step 21 (the exception for a מת מצוה), which is then challenged with 'אמאי' on 20a. Source: Berakhot 19b: לאחותו הוא דאינו מטמא.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 20b, step 21** (hebrewStepName): Step 18–19 brought a proof (תא שמע ... אשה מברכת לבעלה) that women's ברכת המזון is דאורייתא. 'הכא במאי עסקינן' pushes that proof aside: the ברייתא is about a rabbinic amount. It answers a proof, not a challenge, and the step's own relation says it 'removes it as a proof'. Source: Berakhot 20b: אלא הכא במאי עסקינן כגון שאכל שיעורא דרבנן.
  - was: תירוץ
  - now: דחיה
- **Berakhot 21a, step 3** (hebrewStepName): 'והרי ברכת המזון ... ותנן' is an objection to the answer in step 2, built exactly like the 'והרי תפלה ... ותנן' of step 1 (labelled קשיא). It rejects an answer, not a proof, and step 4 gives a new answer (תירוץ). Source: Berakhot 21a: והרי ברכת המזון לאחריו דלית בה מלכות שמים, ותנן.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 21a, step 18** (hebrewStepName): The משנה is quoted to raise the challenge of step 19 ('ואי סלקא דעתך אמת ויציב דאורייתא — לברוך לאחריה!'). It is the source of an objection, not support. Source: Berakhot 21a: תנן: בעל קרי מהרהר בלבו ... ואי סלקא דעתך ״אמת ויציב״ דאורייתא — לברוך לאחריה.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 21a, step 20** (relationToPreviousStep): Step 19 is a challenge (קשיא) and step 20 a תירוץ; the relation calls it a proof. Source: Berakhot 21a: מאי טעמא מברך, אי משום יציאת מצרים — הא אדכר ליה בקריאת שמע.
  - was: Rejects the proof from step 19.
  - now: Answers the challenge from step 19.
- **Berakhot 24a, step 1** (hebrewStepName): Answers the plain question 'מאי טעמא' at the end of 23b (step 30, labelled שאלה); no challenge is being resolved, so it is the תשובה to that שאלה. Source: Berakhot 24a: כֹּל לְנַטּוֹרִינְהוּ טְפֵי עֲדִיף.
  - was: תירוץ
  - now: תשובה
- **Berakhot 24a, step 38** (hebrewStepName): This is the rest of the מיתיבי ברייתא (התולה תפיליו יתלו לו חייו) used to attack רבי's practice; it is part of the challenge, not a proof for any position (its own relation says it 'strengthens the objection'). Source: Berakhot 24a: דּוֹרְשֵׁי חֲמוּרוֹת אָמְרוּ ... זֶה הַתּוֹלֶה תְּפִילָּיו.
  - was: ראיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 24b, step 28** (hebrewStepName): 'אִינִי? וְהָא רַבִּי אֲבָהוּ...' attacks the suggested answer of step 27 with a contradicting story; that is a challenge (אִינִי/וְהָא), not the pushing aside of a proof. Source: Berakhot 24b: אִינִי, וְהָא רַבִּי אֲבָהוּ הֲוָה קָא אָזֵיל בָּתְרֵיהּ דְּרַבִּי יוֹחָנָן.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: קשיא
- **Berakhot 24b, step 28** (stepSummary): Matches the label change: the story is a challenge. Source: Berakhot 24b.
  - was: The answer is pushed away by a story.
  - now: The answer is challenged by a story.
- **Berakhot 24b, step 29** (hebrewStepName): 'הָכִי קָאָמַר לֵיהּ' answers the challenge of step 28 by explaining what רבי יוחנן meant; that resolves a קשיא. Source: Berakhot 24b: הָכִי קָאָמַר לֵיהּ: לְדִידִי לָא סְבִירָא לִי, לְדִידָךְ דִּסְבִירָא לָךְ.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: תירוץ
- **Berakhot 24b, step 29** (relationToPreviousStep): Matches the label change. Source: Berakhot 24b.
  - was: Pushes away the story-based rejection in step 28
  - now: Answers the story-based challenge in step 28
- **Berakhot 24b, step 29** (stepSummary): The story was a challenge, not a proof. Source: Berakhot 24b.
  - was: The proof from the story is pushed away.
  - now: The challenge from the story is answered.
- **Berakhot 25a, step 32** (hebrewStepName): אביי answers רב יוסף's challenge (step 31) by showing רב has a strict statement too; it answers a קשיא, not a proof. Source: Berakhot 25a: אָמַר לֵיהּ אַבָּיֵי: מַאי חָזֵית דְּסָמְכַתְּ אַהָא, סְמוֹךְ אַהָא.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: תירוץ
- **Berakhot 25a, step 32** (relationToPreviousStep): Matches the label change. Source: Berakhot 25a.
  - was: Rejects רב יוסף's challenge
  - now: Answers רב יוסף's challenge
- **Berakhot 25a, step 40** (hebrewStepName): Step 39 is a מיתיבי against רבא; 'וּלְטַעְמָיךְ אֵימָא רֵישָׁא' answers it (its own relation says 'Answers the challenge'), so it is a resolution. Source: Berakhot 25a: וּלְטַעְמָיךְ אֵימָא רֵישָׁא: כׇּל זְמַן שֶׁמַּטְפִּיחִין.
  - was: דחיה
  - now: תירוץ
- **Berakhot 25b, step 29** (hebrewStepName): A short story of רב יוסף's own practice in the middle of the topic (the sugya continues with the chamber-pot ברייתא); it does not settle anything. Source: Berakhot 25b: אֲמַר לֵיהּ רַב יוֹסֵף לְשַׁמָּעֵיהּ: אַיְּיתִי לִי רְבִיעֵיתָא דְמַיָּא.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: מימרא
- **Berakhot 26a, step 1** (hebrewStepName): This is the second half of רבא's statement 'גְּלִימָא אַקַּמְטְרָא...' begun at 25b step 47 (labelled מימרא); it is a ruling by רבא, not where a discussion lands, and is followed by more rulings. Source: Berakhot 25b-26a: אָמַר רָבָא: גְּלִימָא אַקַּמְטְרָא — כִּכְלִי בְּתוֹךְ כְּלִי דָּמֵי.
  - was: מסקנא
  - now: מימרא

## 202611060242-rev94-dafyomi-berakhot-2

Approved: Drafted overnight Nov 5–6 2026 by Claude from a line-by-line check against the Gemara and Rashi on Sefaria (each finding re-checked before applying); David asked for fixes to go live overnight. Awaiting review by a rav.

- **Berakhot 12a, step 21** (stepSummary): Rashi says explicitly that the answer is NOT that the ending works as its own ברכה; the opening itself is valid because it already includes both day and night. Source: Berakhot 12a 'כי קאמר ברכה ומלכות מעיקרא אתרוייהו קאמר'; Rashi s.v. ה"ג אלא כיון דאמר: 'לא תימא משום חתימתה יצא אלא פתיחתה נמי מעלייתא היא'.
  - was: So the name and kingship said at the start apply to both parts, and the ending is still a proper ברכה even for רבי יוחנן.
  - now: So the name and kingship said at the start cover both day and night, and the opening itself is valid, not only the ending. So the ברייתא still proves nothing about wine and beer.
- **Berakhot 12a, step 21** (whatsHappening): Same error: per Rashi the opening is valid on its own, not carried over to make the ending a full ברכה. Source: Rashi 12a s.v. ה"ג אלא כיון דאמר: 'הלכך אדעתא דתרוייהו הויא'.
  - was: The kingship at the opening of the ברכה counts for the ending too.
  - now: The opening, said with God's name and kingship, already fits both day and night, so it is a valid opening at either time.
- **Berakhot 12a, step 21** (deeperAnalysis): Same error: Rashi rejects reading this as the ending becoming a full ברכה. Source: Rashi 12a s.v. ה"ג אלא כיון דאמר.
  - was: the opening kingship applies to the whole ברכה. So for רבי יוחנן the ending can also act as a full ברכה, and there is still no proof
  - now: an opening said with the other time of day in mind still fits, so he is יוצא because the opening is valid too, not only because of the ending (Rashi). So there is still no proof
- **Berakhot 12a, step 21** (phrases.5.notes): Same error shown on screen in the phrase note. Source: Rashi 12a s.v. ה"ג אלא כיון דאמר.
  - was: so the opening kingship carries over to the ending too.
  - now: so an opening meant for the other time of day is still a valid opening.
- **Berakhot 13a, step 18** (stepSummary): 'Had in mind to do so' means intent to fulfill the מצוה, but the גמרא concludes it means intent to read (בקורא להגיה); this also contradicts the step's own confusionAlert. Source: Berakhot 13a 'מאי אם כוון לבו — לקרות... בקורא להגיה'; Rashi s.v. בקורא להגיה.
  - was: fulfills his duty if he had in mind to do so.
  - now: fulfills his duty if he focused his heart (כוון לבו).
- **Berakhot 16b, step 1** (simpleTranslation): The בתולה/אלמנה distinction comes from the ברייתא on 16a (מכאן אמרו), not from the משנה, which only says a חתן is exempt. Source: Berakhot 16a: מִכָּאן אָמְרוּ: הַכּוֹנֵס אֶת הַבְּתוּלָה — פָּטוּר (ברייתא, תנו רבנן); משנה 16a: חָתָן פָּטוּר מִקְּרִיאַת שְׁמַע.
  - was: why does the משנה talk specifically about someone marrying a בתולה?
  - now: why does the ברייתא talk specifically about someone marrying a בתולה?
- **Berakhot 16b, step 1** (deeperAnalysis): Same error: the בתולה case is in the ברייתא, not the משנה. Source: Berakhot 16a: תָּנוּ רַבָּנַן ... מִכָּאן אָמְרוּ: הַכּוֹנֵס אֶת הַבְּתוּלָה — פָּטוּר.
  - was: the משנה's focus on a בתולה looks too narrow
  - now: the ברייתא's focus on a בתולה looks too narrow
- **Berakhot 16b, step 2** (deeperAnalysis): Same error: the משנה never mentions a בתולה; the ברייתא does. Source: Berakhot 16a: הַכּוֹנֵס אֶת הַבְּתוּלָה — פָּטוּר, וְאֶת הָאַלְמָנָה — חַיָּיב (ברייתא).
  - was: That is why the משנה mentions only the בתולה case.
  - now: That is why the ברייתא exempts only the בתולה case.
- **Berakhot 17a** (overview): Rashi says the חכמים took leave of one another (זה מזה) to go home, not that students blessed their רבי. Source: Berakhot 17a: כִּי הֲווֹ מִפַּטְרִי רַבָּנַן מִבֵּי רַבִּי אַמֵּי; Rashi s.v. מפטרי רבנן: זה מזה שהיה נוטל רשות מחבירו לשוב לביתו ולארצו.
  - was: It ends with the farewell blessings students gave their רבי,
  - now: It ends with the farewell blessings the חכמים gave each other when leaving the ישיבה,
- **Berakhot 17a, step 18** (stepSummary): Per Rashi the blessing was said by the חכמים to one another on parting, not to רבי אמי. Source: Rashi 17a s.v. מפטרי רבנן: זה מזה שהיה נוטל רשות מחבירו.
  - was: they gave him a long blessing
  - now: they gave each other a long blessing
- **Berakhot 17a, step 18** (simpleTranslation): Per Rashi the חכמים blessed one another on parting. Source: Rashi 17a s.v. מפטרי רבנן: זה מזה.
  - was: they would bless him like this:
  - now: they would bless each other like this:
- **Berakhot 17a, step 18** (whatsHappening): Rashi: the parting was of colleagues from each other, not students from their teacher. Source: Rashi 17a s.v. מפטרי רבנן: זה מזה שהיה נוטל רשות מחבירו לשוב לביתו ולארצו.
  - was: the blessing that students gave their רבי when leaving his ישיבה
  - now: the blessing the חכמים gave each other when they took leave of one another at the ישיבה to go home
- **Berakhot 17a, step 18** (mimraCoreRuling): Per Rashi the blessing was between colleagues on parting. Source: Rashi 17a s.v. מפטרי רבנן: זה מזה.
  - was: Students would bless their רבי with
  - now: When the חכמים parted from each other, they would bless one another with
- **Berakhot 17a, step 19** (stepSummary): Per Rashi the farewell was between the חכמים themselves. Source: Rashi 17a s.v. מפטרי רבנן: זה מזה.
  - was: they blessed him with a פסוק
  - now: they blessed each other with a פסוק
- **Berakhot 17a, step 19** (simpleTranslation): Per Rashi the farewell was between the חכמים themselves. Source: Rashi 17a s.v. מפטרי רבנן: זה מזה.
  - was: they would say to him the פסוק
  - now: they would say to each other the פסוק
- **Berakhot 17a, step 19** (whatToRemember): Per Rashi the farewell blessings were between colleagues, not to the רבי. Source: Rashi 17a s.v. מפטרי רבנן: זה מזה שהיה נוטל רשות מחבירו.
  - was: The חכמים had a custom of blessing their רבי when they left his ישיבה,
  - now: The חכמים had a custom of blessing each other when they parted at the ישיבה,
- **Berakhot 17a, step 19** (whatsHappening): Per Rashi the farewell blessings were said by the חכמים to one another, not by students to a teacher. Source: Rashi 17a s.v. מפטרי רבנן: זה מזה.
  - was: Here the students used a פסוק
  - now: Here the חכמים used a פסוק
- **Berakhot 19a, step 17** (whatsHappening): In רבי אלעזר's list, hand-washing comes first and speaking after the bier of scholars comes second; the Gemara explains the second one first. Source: Berakhot 19a: נפק דק ואשכח תלת: המזלזל בנטילת ידים, והמספר אחר מטתן של תלמידי חכמים, והמגיס דעתו.
  - was: takes the first of רבי אלעזר's three cases
  - now: takes the second case on רבי אלעזר's list (it is explained first)
- **Berakhot 19a, step 19** (whatsHappening): Belittling hand-washing is the first case in רבי אלעזר's list, not the second. Source: Berakhot 19a: ואשכח תלת: המזלזל בנטילת ידים....
  - was: moves to the second case and asks
  - now: moves to the first case on רבי אלעזר's list and asks
- **Berakhot 21a, step 1** (stepSummary): The reason being challenged, 'a matter the community is busy with', is רב אדא בר אהבה's statement at the end of 20b; רבי אלעזר's reason was only 'so he does not sit idle'. Source: Berakhot 20b: אמר רב אדא בר אהבה בדבר שהציבור עוסקין בו; 21a: והרי תפלה דדבר שהציבור עסוקין בו.
  - was: challenges רבי אלעזר's reason using תפלה
  - now: challenges רב אדא בר אהבה's reason using תפלה
- **Berakhot 21a, step 1** (relationToPreviousStep): Same misattribution: 'because the community is busy with it' is רב אדא בר אהבה's answer, not רבי אלעזר's. Source: Berakhot 20b: אמר רב אדא בר אהבה בדבר שהציבור עוסקין בו.
  - was: Challenges רבי אלעזר's reason from before this section
  - now: Challenges רב אדא בר אהבה's reason from before this section
- **Berakhot 22b** (overview): The four-אמות distance in the משנה is from urine and feces, not from foul things in general; the משנה is elliptical and 'from them' means urine. Source: Berakhot 22b mishna (וכמה ירחיק מהן ומן הצואה); Rashi s.v. וכמה ירחיק מהם – ממי רגלים, and s.v. עד שיתן לתוכן מים (חסורי מחסרא, explained on 25b).
  - was: and to keep four אמות from foul things
  - now: and to keep four אמות from urine and צואה
- **Berakhot 22b, step 22** (stepSummary): Per Rashi and the Gemara on 25b, 'מהן' in the משנה refers to urine, not to the foul or soaking water. Source: Berakhot 22b (וכמה ירחיק מהן ומן הצואה – ארבע אמות); Rashi s.v. וכמה ירחיק מהם – ממי רגלים.
  - was: One must also keep four אמות away from such things and from צואה.
  - now: One must also keep four אמות away from urine and from צואה.
- **Berakhot 22b, step 22** (simpleTranslation): 'Them' is urine according to Rashi's reading of the elliptical משנה, not the foul water just mentioned. Source: Rashi s.v. וכמה ירחיק מהם – ממי רגלים.
  - was: And how far must he keep from them and from feces?
  - now: And how far must he keep from them (urine) and from feces?
- **Berakhot 22b, step 22** (deeperAnalysis): The distance rule is about urine and feces, per Rashi. Source: Rashi s.v. וכמה ירחיק מהם – ממי רגלים.
  - was: keep four אמות from bad-smelling things and צואה when saying שמע
  - now: keep four אמות from urine and צואה when saying שמע
- **Berakhot 22b, step 22** (whatToRemember): The distance rule is about urine and feces, per Rashi. Source: Rashi s.v. וכמה ירחיק מהם – ממי רגלים.
  - was: and keep four אמות from foul things.
  - now: and keep four אמות from urine and צואה.
- **Berakhot 22b, step 22** (mimraCoreRuling): The distance rule is about urine and feces, per Rashi. Source: Rashi s.v. וכמה ירחיק מהם – ממי רגלים.
  - was: One must keep four אמות from foul things and צואה.
  - now: One must keep four אמות from urine and צואה.
- **Berakhot 23a, step 15** (deeperAnalysis): The ברייתא only requires removing them from the head; the same amud (steps 19-26) concludes that he holds them in his hand and enters, so saying they may not be brought in contradicts the site's own later steps. Source: Berakhot 23a (חולץ תפיליו ברחוק ארבע אמות ונכנס; התקינו שיהא אוחזן בידו ונכנס); Rashi s.v. חולץ ונפנה לאלתר – ובקבוע נמי אוחזן בידו... שבזמן שהם בראשו הם בגלוי.
  - was: you may not bring תפילין into a bathroom, and you must take them off before you get close to it
  - now: you may not wear תפילין into a bathroom, so you must take them off before you get close to it
- **Berakhot 23b, step 5** (phrases.3.notes): The line is from a separate ברייתא introduced by מיתיבי (whose סיפא has 'זהו קל וחומר שאין עליו תשובה'); it does not appear in the bathroom ברייתות on 23a. Source: Berakhot 23b (מיתיבי: דברים שהתרתי לך כאן...); Rashi s.v. הא דקתני עלה – בסיפא דידה.
  - was: This line comes from the earlier bathroom ברייתא.
  - now: This line comes from a ברייתא about the two kinds of bathroom.
- **Berakhot 23b, step 5** (whatsHappening): The quoted line is not from the 23a bathroom ברייתות; it is a new source brought with מיתיבי. Source: Berakhot 23b (מיתיבי: דברים שהתרתי לך כאן אסרתי לך כאן).
  - was: using a line from the earlier bathroom ברייתא.
  - now: using a line from a ברייתא about the two kinds of bathroom.
- **Berakhot 25a, step 12** (stepSummary): The condition 'when hides were placed in them' applies to both pig and dog waste, not only to dog waste. Source: Berakhot 25a: וְלֹא כְּנֶגֶד צוֹאַת חֲזִירִים וְלֹא כְּנֶגֶד צוֹאַת כְּלָבִים בִּזְמַן שֶׁנָּתַן עוֹרוֹת לְתוֹכָן; Rashi s.v. בזמן שיש בהן עורות: אצואת כלבים וחזירים קאי; Rashi s.v. לית הלכתא: דאסר צואת כלבים וחזירים בשאין בהם עורות.
  - was: forbids only human waste, pig waste, and dog waste that hides were put into.
  - now: forbids only human waste, and pig or dog waste that hides were put into.
- **Berakhot 25a, step 12** (simpleTranslation): Same error: the hides condition covers both pig and dog waste (לְתוֹכָן is plural). Source: Berakhot 25a: בִּזְמַן שֶׁנָּתַן עוֹרוֹת לְתוֹכָן; Rashi s.v. בזמן שיש בהן עורות.
  - was: or pig waste, or dog waste when hides were placed in it.
  - now: or pig waste or dog waste when hides were placed in them.
- **Berakhot 25a, step 12** (deeperAnalysis): Rashi says רבא rejects the first ברייתא for forbidding dog and pig waste without hides; the second ברייתא's hides condition applies to both. Source: Rashi 25a s.v. לית הלכתא כי הא מתניתא: דאסר צואת כלבים וחזירים בשאין בהם עורות.
  - was: The first ברייתא forbade all dog waste, chicken waste, and a smelly garbage heap. The second ברייתא forbids only human waste, pig waste, and dog waste used for tanning hides.
  - now: The first ברייתא forbade all dog and pig waste, chicken waste, and a smelly garbage heap. The second ברייתא forbids only human waste, and pig or dog waste used for tanning hides.
- **Berakhot 25a, step 12** (mimraCoreRuling): The hides condition applies to pig waste too, not only dog waste. Source: Rashi 25a s.v. בזמן שיש בהן עורות: אצואת כלבים וחזירים קאי.
  - was: human waste, pig waste, or dog waste that hides were placed in.
  - now: human waste, or pig or dog waste that hides were placed in.
- **Berakhot 25a, step 12** (phrases.8.english): לְתוֹכָן is plural and refers to both the pig and dog waste. Source: Berakhot 25a: בִּזְמַן שֶׁנָּתַן עוֹרוֹת לְתוֹכָן; Rashi s.v. בזמן שיש בהן עורות.
  - was: when he placed hides into it.
  - now: when he placed hides into them.
- **Berakhot 24a, step 17** (deeperAnalysis): Rashi explains the problem as the two people's buttocks touching each other, not facing each other (once turned away, neither can see the other). Source: Berakhot 24a: וְהָא אִיכָּא עֲגָבוֹת; Rashi s.v. הא איכא עגבות: דנגעי אהדדי.
  - was: but now the two backs face each other.
  - now: but now their bare buttocks touch each other.
- **Berakhot 24a, step 17** (phrases.3.notes): Same point: per Rashi the concern is contact between their buttocks. Source: Rashi 24a s.v. הא איכא עגבות: דנגעי אהדדי.
  - was: their bare backs are facing each other.
  - now: their bare buttocks touch each other.
- **Berakhot 26a, step 1** (title): The line is the end of רבא's ruling that begins on 25b with גְּלִימָא (a cloak); it is the cloak over the chest, not merely placing the item on a chest, that counts as a vessel within a vessel. Source: Berakhot 25b-26a: אָמַר רָבָא: גְּלִימָא אַקַּמְטְרָא — כִּכְלִי בְּתוֹךְ כְּלִי דָּמֵי; Rashi s.v. ככלי בתוך כלי דמי: דהא גלימא לאו כליין הוא.
  - was: Placing it on a chest counts as a vessel within a vessel
  - now: A cloak spread over a chest counts as a vessel within a vessel
- **Berakhot 26a, step 1** (stepSummary): Omits the cloak (גְּלִימָא) that starts רבא's ruling on 25b; placing on a chest alone is not what the Gemara says counts as double covering. Source: Berakhot 25b: אָמַר רָבָא: גְּלִימָא אַקַּמְטְרָא; Rashi s.v. ככלי בתוך כלי דמי.
  - was: an item placed on top of a chest is treated like a vessel inside another vessel
  - now: according to רבא, a cloak spread over the items on a chest is treated like a vessel inside another vessel
- **Berakhot 26a, step 1** (simpleTranslation): The subject of the sentence is the cloak from the end of 25b, not the holy item itself. Source: Berakhot 25b: אָמַר רָבָא: גְּלִימָא אַקַּמְטְרָא.
  - was: If it is placed on a chest, that is like a vessel within a vessel.
  - now: (רבא said: a cloak) on a chest is like a vessel within a vessel.
- **Berakhot 26a, step 1** (whatsHappening): Same error: the double covering is cloak plus chest, not the chest alone. Source: Berakhot 25b-26a; Rashi s.v. ככלי בתוך כלי דמי: דהא גלימא לאו כליין הוא.
  - was: Putting a holy object on top of a chest counts as covering it twice, like a vessel inside a vessel.
  - now: רבא's ruling: a cloak spread over holy items on a chest counts as covering them twice, like a vessel inside a vessel.
- **Berakhot 26a, step 1** (deeperAnalysis): Same error; Rashi explains the cloak counts because it is not the items' own container (כליין). Source: Rashi s.v. ככלי בתוך כלי דמי: דהא גלימא לאו כליין הוא.
  - was: The new point is that the chest adds a second layer.
  - now: The new point is that a cloak over a chest gives two layers; since a cloak is not the items' own designated container, it counts as a real second covering.
- **Berakhot 26a, step 1** (phrases.1.english): The phrase continues רבא's sentence from 25b whose subject is the cloak. Source: Berakhot 25b: אָמַר רָבָא: גְּלִימָא.
  - was: On a chest, it is like a vessel within a vessel.
  - now: (A cloak) on a chest is like a vessel within a vessel. (The word 'cloak,' גְּלִימָא, is at the end of the previous amud.)
- **Arakhin 8b, step 16** (deeperAnalysis): Rashi explains נושא as God lifting the pan of sins so the merits tip the scale, not lifting the person. Source: Arakhin 8b רבי יוסי ברבי חנינא אמר: נושא; Rashi s.v. נושא: נושא ומגביה כף העונות למעלה כדי שיכריעו הזכיות; Rashi s.v. ורב יהודה: שאתה מגביה עונות.
  - was: so He lifts the person up, like רבי יוסי ברבי חנינא's נושא.
  - now: so He lifts up the side of the scale holding the sins, letting the merits outweigh them, like רבי יוסי ברבי חנינא's נושא.
- **Arakhin 8b, step 16** (whatToRemember): Same error as in deeperAnalysis: per Rashi, נושא means lifting the pan of sins, not lifting the person. Source: Arakhin 8b; Rashi s.v. נושא: נושא ומגביה כף העונות למעלה.
  - was: (God lifts the person up, high as 'mighty mountains')
  - now: (God lifts up the scale of sins so the merits outweigh them)
- **Zevachim 85a, step 21** (whatsHappening): Per Rashi the animal stays on the מזבח and is burned there (אם עלו לא ירדו); the reason it is flayed in place is that once taken down it could not go back up, not that it can no longer be offered. Source: Zevachim 85a; Rashi s.v. הכא במאי עסקינן (דאי מחית לה תו לא סלקא) and s.v. כגון דהיתה לו שעת הכושר (השתא נמי כי איתא בראש המזבח ונקטרת בעיא הפשט ונתוח).
  - was: Since it can no longer be offered, it is flayed right where it is.
  - now: Since it may not go back up if it is taken down, it is flayed right where it is, on top of the מזבח.
- **Zevachim 85a, step 1** (deeperAnalysis): 'The inner מזבח' means the golden incense altar; the point is the outer altar in the courtyard, where animal offerings go. Source: Zevachim 85a (לא תהא פחותה משוחט בחוץ ומעלה בחוץ); Rashi s.v. לא תהא פחותה.
  - was: could never be brought on the inner מזבח
  - now: could never be brought on the מזבח in the עזרה
- **Temurah 27a** (overview): Only רב אשי says it depends on where the hand rests; רבא said only that a blemished animal allows the 'moving holiness' meaning. Source: Temurah 27a: אמר רבא ... כגון שהיה הקדש בעל מום; אמר רב אשי ... ידו אקודש חול הוי ידו אחול קודש הוי.
  - was: רבא and רב אשי add that a blemished holy animal can go either way, depending on which animal the owner's hand rests on.
  - now: רבא adds that with a blemished holy animal, תחת can mean moving the holiness, and רב אשי adds that it can go either way, depending on which animal the owner's hand rests on.
- **Bekhorot 51a, step 11** (deeperAnalysis): The site gives a Torah-level reason (a note is not money), but Rashi and עולא on 51b say that by Torah law the son is redeemed and the rule is only a גזירה lest people say one redeems with a שטר. Source: Rashi 51a s.v. ובנו אינו פדוי: כדאמרינן בגמרא שמא יאמרו פודין בשטרות; Bekhorot 51b: אמר עולא דבר תורה בנו פדוי ... גזירה שמא יאמרו פודין בשטר.
  - was: But a note is not money in hand, so it does not redeem the son.
  - now: The son is still not redeemed. Rashi explains, as the גמרא says on the next amud, that this is a rabbinic decree, so that people will not say one may redeem with שטרות.
- **Bekhorot 53a, step 25** (stepSummary): Rashi says the proposal is to sell the mothers' ears to a non-Jew, so that a non-Jew is a partner and the offspring never become בכור; the site says the unborn animals' ears. Source: Rashi 53a s.v. הכי קאמר: לקנינהו לאודנייהו דאימהות לעובדי כוכבים כי היכי דתיהוי יד עובד כוכבים באמצע.
  - was: sell the ears of the unborn animals
  - now: sell the ears of the mother animals
- **Bekhorot 53b, step 18** (deeperAnalysis): Rashi explains the משנה as switching between מזבח holiness and בדק הבית holiness (the stringent holiness being the מזבח's), which is also how step 19 and the גמרא's 'not every animal stands to be an עולה' work; the site's example of a light קרבן turned into an עולה is not Rashi's case. Source: Rashi 53b s.v. מקדושה לקדושה: שאין קדושת מזבח חלה על קדושת בדה"ב ואע"ג דקדושת מזבח חמורה; s.v. מהו דתימא.
  - was: For example, an animal that is already a קרבן of light holiness cannot be turned into a more stringent קרבן such as an עולה.
  - now: For example, an animal already consecrated for בדק הבית cannot then be made a קרבן for the מזבח, even though the holiness of the מזבח is more stringent.
- **Bekhorot 54b, step 19** (deeperAnalysis): Rashi says explicitly that the משנה's 'he brings them and tithes them in the middle' does not mean he literally brings them; the middle flock simply joins the two sides. Source: Rashi 54b s.v. היה לו באמצע: אותן האמצעיות מצרפות את הצדדין והאי דקתני מביא ומעשרן באמצע לא מביא ממש קאמר.
  - was: The owner gathers all of them in the middle and tithes them together.
  - now: The middle animals join all of them into one group for the tithe. Rashi notes that 'brings them to the middle' is not meant literally.
- **Bekhorot 52a, step 7** (deeperAnalysis): The site follows the תוספות reading (his own daughters, fed under the בית דין condition of the כתובה) without saying that Rashi reads it differently; the reading itself is sound, so only a note is needed. Source: Rashi 52a s.v. ולא הבנות: שהיה מקבל עליו לזון את בתה שהיה לה מאיש אחר חמש שנים; Tosafot 52a s.v. והאמר שמואל: ובקונטרס פירש מזון הבנות כגון נשא אשה ופסק לזון בתה ... אלא במזון הבת שהוא תנאי ב"ד איירי (Ketubot 52b בנן נוקבן; Ketubot 101b הנושא את האשה ופסקה עמו שיזון את בתה).
  - was: After a man dies, his daughters are supported from his estate.
  - now: After a man dies, his daughters are supported from his estate. (This follows תוספות. Rashi explains the case as a wife's daughter from another man, whom the husband agreed to feed for five years.)
- **Bekhorot 57a, step 4** (deeperAnalysis): Rashi explains all ten are forbidden because one unknown lamb among them is the dog's price, not because all ten count as its payment; the site's version also undercuts the next step's question (why not pick one). Source: Bekhorot 57a (שֶׁכְּנֶגֶד הַכֶּלֶב אֲסוּרִין); Rashi s.v. אחד נטל עשרה (דאחד מהן היה חילופו של כלב ... ולא ידעינן הי ניהו).
  - was: The ברייתא treats all ten lambs as payment for the dog, so all ten are forbidden.
  - now: One of the ten lambs is the dog's price, but we do not know which one, so all ten are forbidden.
- **Bekhorot 57b, step 9** (stepSummary): Rashi learns the question from the word נעלסה itself (the bird carries its egg up and lowers it gently), not from a later verse about leaving eggs on the ground. Source: Bekhorot 57b (וְהָא כְּתִיב: ״כְּנַף רְנָנִים נֶעֱלָסָה״); Rashi s.v. והכתיב כנף רננים נעלסה (נושא עולה ונתחטא ... ומורידה בנחת בקינה אלמא דלא שדיא ליה).
  - was: since a פסוק says this bird leaves its eggs on the ground.
  - now: since a פסוק shows that this bird lifts its egg and sets it down gently in its nest.
- **Bekhorot 57b, step 9** (simpleTranslation): Same error: the question is from נעלסה as Rashi reads it. Source: Bekhorot 57b (וְהָא כְּתִיב: ״כְּנַף רְנָנִים נֶעֱלָסָה״); Rashi s.v. והכתיב כנף רננים נעלסה (נושא עולה ונתחטא ... ומורידה בנחת בקינה אלמא דלא שדיא ליה).
  - was: (and the פסוק goes on to say it leaves its eggs on the ground)
  - now: (Rashi: this means the bird lifts its egg and lowers it gently, so it does not drop it)
- **Bekhorot 57b, step 9** (whatsHappening): Same error: the question is from נעלסה as Rashi reads it. Source: Bekhorot 57b (וְהָא כְּתִיב: ״כְּנַף רְנָנִים נֶעֱלָסָה״); Rashi s.v. והכתיב כנף רננים נעלסה (נושא עולה ונתחטא ... ומורידה בנחת בקינה אלמא דלא שדיא ליה).
  - was: A פסוק in איוב describes this bird as laying its eggs on the earth, so how could an egg fall from high up?
  - now: A פסוק in איוב praises this bird: Rashi reads נעלסה as 'it carries up and lowers gently,' meaning the bird sets its huge egg down carefully in its nest. So how could an egg fall?
- **Bekhorot 57b, step 9** (deeperAnalysis): Same error: the question is from נעלסה as Rashi reads it. Source: Bekhorot 57b (וְהָא כְּתִיב: ״כְּנַף רְנָנִים נֶעֱלָסָה״); Rashi s.v. והכתיב כנף רננים נעלסה (נושא עולה ונתחטא ... ומורידה בנחת בקינה אלמא דלא שדיא ליה).
  - was: The פסוק continues: 'for she leaves her eggs on the earth.' If the eggs are laid on the ground, it is hard to explain a falling egg.
  - now: Rashi reads נעלסה as initials: נושא, עולה, ונתחטא, the bird lifts the egg, goes up, and brings it down gently. A bird that handles its egg so carefully does not drop it.
- **Bekhorot 57b, step 9** (confusionAlert): Same error: the confusionAlert asserts the non-Rashi reading as the only one. Source: Bekhorot 57b (וְהָא כְּתִיב: ״כְּנַף רְנָנִים נֶעֱלָסָה״); Rashi s.v. והכתיב כנף רננים נעלסה (נושא עולה ונתחטא ... ומורידה בנחת בקינה אלמא דלא שדיא ליה).
  - was: The part that actually creates the question, 'she leaves her eggs on the earth' (איוב 39:14), is in the next verse of איוב. The גמרא does not quote it and assumes the learner knows it.
  - now: Rashi finds the question in the word נעלסה itself, read as 'carries up and lowers gently.' (Some explain it instead from the next verse, 'she leaves her eggs on the earth,' איוב 39:14.)
- **Bekhorot 57b, step 9** (phrases.3.notes): Same error, in the phrase note (this field is phrases.3.notes, not english). Source: Bekhorot 57b (וְהָא כְּתִיב: ״כְּנַף רְנָנִים נֶעֱלָסָה״); Rashi s.v. והכתיב כנף רננים נעלסה (נושא עולה ונתחטא ... ומורידה בנחת בקינה אלמא דלא שדיא ליה).
  - was: The verse continues: 'she leaves her eggs on the earth' (איוב 39:13–14).
  - now: Rashi: נעלסה means the bird lifts its egg and lowers it gently into its nest, so it does not drop it.
- **Bekhorot 57b, step 10** (whatsHappening): Keeps step 10 consistent with Rashi's reading of the question in step 9. Source: Bekhorot 57b (וְהָא כְּתִיב: ״כְּנַף רְנָנִים נֶעֱלָסָה״); Rashi s.v. והכתיב כנף רננים נעלסה (נושא עולה ונתחטא ... ומורידה בנחת בקינה אלמא דלא שדיא ליה); Rashi s.v. מוזרתא הואי (שלא היתה ראויה לאפרוח לכך השליכה).
  - was: Normally the bird lays its eggs on the ground, but
  - now: Normally the bird sets its eggs down carefully, but
- **Bekhorot 58a, step 19** (stepSummary): Rashi says outright that on א׳ אלול there is no real mixing (the אלול-born are still too young to be tithed); the reason is only the need for a clear marker between new and old. Source: Bekhorot 58a (וּבָעֵינַן לְמִיעְבַּד הֶכֵּירָא בְּחָדָשׁ וְיָשָׁן); Rashi s.v. ובעינן היכירא (דנהי דליכא למימר דליתו לעשורי הנהו דאלול והנהו דאב בהדדי ... היכירא בעינן).
  - was: Tithing on that day would mix the new animals born in אלול with the old ones born in אב.
  - now: No אלול-born animal could be tithed yet on that day, but a clear marker (היכירא) is needed so that the old (אב) animals are never tithed once the new month has begun.
- **Bekhorot 58b, step 3** (deeperAnalysis): Rashi (and the Steinsaltz English) say the challenge is about the five תשרי animals joining a later גורן of the same year; the אב animals belong to the earlier year and could not join a later round. Source: Bekhorot 58b (לִצְטַרְפוּ לְגוֹרֶן אַחֵר); Rashi s.v. מאי אמרת לצטרפו (הנך חמשה דתשרי לגורן אחר).
  - was: free the leftover אב or תשרי animals completely
  - now: free the leftover תשרי animals completely

## 202611060236-rev93-dafyomi-berakhot-1

Approved: Drafted overnight Nov 5–6 2026 by Claude from a line-by-line check against the Gemara and Rashi on Sefaria (each finding re-checked before applying); David asked for fixes to go live overnight. Awaiting review by a rav.

- **Berakhot 2b, step 1** (stepSummary): The site gives only Tosafos' reading (start of sunset) as if it were the plain meaning; Rashi reads ביאת אורו as the sun's light rising on the eighth morning, with the man becoming pure by bringing his קרבנות, and the site does not say it is following another view. Source: Berakhot 2b ("דילמא ביאת אורו הוא ומאי וטהר טהר גברא"); Rashi s.v. דילמא ביאת אורו: "שיאור השמש ביום השמיני ויטהר האיש עצמו בהבאת קרבנותיו"; Tosafos s.v. דילמא ביאת אורו quotes Rashi and offers the start-of-sunset reading.
  - was: could mean the sun's light starts to fade, and "וטהר" could mean the man becomes pure.
  - now: could mean the sun's light starts to fade (so תוספות; רש"י reads it as the sun's light rising the next morning, the eighth day), and "וטהר" could mean the man becomes pure (רש"י: by bringing his קרבנות).
- **Berakhot 2b, step 1** (deeperAnalysis): The site gives only Tosafos' reading (start of sunset) as if it were the plain meaning; Rashi reads ביאת אורו as the sun's light rising on the eighth morning, with the man becoming pure by bringing his קרבנות, and the site does not say it is following another view. Source: Berakhot 2b ("דילמא ביאת אורו הוא ומאי וטהר טהר גברא"); Rashi s.v. דילמא ביאת אורו: "שיאור השמש ביום השמיני ויטהר האיש עצמו בהבאת קרבנותיו"; Tosafos s.v. דילמא ביאת אורו quotes Rashi and offers the start-of-sunset reading.
  - was: That would also move the start time of evening קריאת שמע earlier.
  - now: That would also move the start time of evening קריאת שמע earlier. (This follows תוספות. רש"י explains the challenge differently: 'ביאת אורו' is the sun's light rising the next morning, the eighth day, and 'וטהר' means the man becomes pure by bringing his קרבנות, so he could not eat תרומה until then.)
- **Berakhot 2b, step 1** (phrases.1.notes): The site gives only Tosafos' reading (start of sunset) as if it were the plain meaning; Rashi reads ביאת אורו as the sun's light rising on the eighth morning, with the man becoming pure by bringing his קרבנות, and the site does not say it is following another view. Source: Berakhot 2b ("דילמא ביאת אורו הוא ומאי וטהר טהר גברא"); Rashi s.v. דילמא ביאת אורו: "שיאור השמש ביום השמיני ויטהר האיש עצמו בהבאת קרבנותיו"; Tosafos s.v. דילמא ביאת אורו quotes Rashi and offers the start-of-sunset reading.
  - was: He means the very start of sunset, when the sun's light begins to go, not full nightfall.
  - now: So תוספות: the very start of sunset, when the sun's light begins to go, not full nightfall. רש"י reads it as the sun's light rising the next morning, the eighth day, when the man brings his קרבנות and only then eats.
- **Berakhot 2b, step 3** (phrases.6.english): The site gives only Tosafos' reading (start of sunset) as if it were the plain meaning; Rashi reads ביאת אורו as the sun's light rising on the eighth morning, with the man becoming pure by bringing his קרבנות, and the site does not say it is following another view. Source: Berakhot 2b ("דילמא ביאת אורו הוא ומאי וטהר טהר גברא"); Rashi s.v. דילמא ביאת אורו: "שיאור השמש ביום השמיני ויטהר האיש עצמו בהבאת קרבנותיו"; Tosafos s.v. דילמא ביאת אורו quotes Rashi and offers the start-of-sunset reading.
  - was: or perhaps it is the setting of its light,
  - now: or perhaps it is the setting of its light (so תוספות; רש"י: the rising of its light the next morning),
- **Berakhot 2b, step 15** (whatToRemember): Step 15's answer is not final: steps 16-21 overturn it and conclude the poor man's time is separate from and later than the כהן's, so calling it 'the final picture' contradicts the amud's own conclusion. Source: Berakhot 2b: "אלא לאו שמע מינה שיעורא דעני לחוד ושיעורא דכהן לחוד שמע מינה ... דעני מאוחר".
  - was: The final picture: poor man = כהן eating תרומה (stars coming out).
  - now: The picture at this stage (overturned in steps 16-21): poor man = כהן eating תרומה (stars coming out).
- **Berakhot 3b, step 39** (phrases.2.notes): The note gives only the Geonim's explanation (rain into its mouth) without saying so; Rashi explains חוליתו as the earth dug out of the pit itself. Source: Berakhot 3b "ואין הבור מתמלא מחוליתו"; Rashi s.v. אין הבור מתמלא מחוליתו: "העוקר חוליא מבור כרוי וחוזר ומשליכו לתוכו אין מתמלא בכך"; Tosafos ibid. (Rabbeinu Tam: מנביעתו).
  - was: A pit can't fill up just from the rain that drops straight into its mouth. Water has to be brought in from outside.
  - now: Rashi: if you dig earth out of a pit and throw it back in, the pit does not fill up. So too, the nation cannot feed its poor from its own resources; food must come from outside. (Other explanations: its own spring water, or rain falling into its mouth, is not enough.)
- **Berakhot 5b** (overview): The overview says אבא בנימין prayed close to bedtime, but step 30 (following רש״י) explains 'סמוך למטתי' as davening right after rising, before any work or learning; the overview contradicts the step and רש״י. Source: Berakhot 5b ״אֶלָּא אֵימָא סָמוּךְ לְמִטָּתִי״; Rashi s.v. סמוך למטתי: ״שלא לעשות מלאכה ושלא לעסוק בתורה כשעמדתי ממטתי עד שאקרא ק"ש ואתפלל״.
  - was: praying close to bedtime
  - now: praying right after getting out of bed (רש״י's reading of 'close to my bed')
- **Berakhot 6b** (overview): The overview puts אביי's limit (only when facing away) into רב הונא's mouth; רב הונא's statement has no such condition, and the step-by-step content correctly credits the limit to אביי. Source: Berakhot 6b: אמר רב הונא כל המתפלל אחורי בית הכנסת נקרא רשע ... אמר אביי לא אמרן אלא דלא מהדר אפיה לבי כנישתא.
  - was: רב הונא calls one who prays behind the בית הכנסת facing away a רשע
  - now: רב הונא calls one who prays behind the בית הכנסת a רשע, and אביי limits this to one who faces away from it
- **Berakhot 9a, step 1** (whatsHappening): The question being answered here (the sunrise ברייתא, end of 8b) is the reverse: the ברייתא calls the time 'day' yet counts the reading as the NIGHT שמע; the site describes the first ברייתא's question instead. Source: Berakhot 8b-9a: אמרת שתי פעמים ביום, אלמא קודם הנץ החמה יממא הוא... אלמא ליליא הוא; Rashi 9a s.v. והאי דקרו ליה ליליא – דקאמר יוצא בו ק"ש של לילה.
  - was: How can the ברייתא call the time after עמוד השחר 'night' and also say that reading then counts for the daytime שמע?
  - now: How can the second ברייתא say both readings are 'in the day', which makes the time before sunrise day, and also count the reading then as the nighttime שמע?
- **Berakhot 9a, step 1** (deeperAnalysis): In this ברייתא the reading before sunrise counts as the NIGHT שמע (the one after sunrise is the day reading); the site says it counts as the daytime שמע, which contradicts the next sentence and Rashi. Source: Berakhot 8b: אחת קודם הנץ החמה ואחת לאחר הנץ החמה... אחת של יום ואחת של לילה; Rashi 9a s.v. והאי דקרו ליה ליליא – דקאמר יוצא בו ק"ש של לילה; s.v. משום דאיכא דגנו – וקרינא ביה ובשכבך.
  - was: legally, after עמוד השחר it is truly day, so in the ברייתא the reading then counts as the daytime שמע. It is called 'night' only
  - now: legally, the time before sunrise (after עמוד השחר) is truly day, which is why the ברייתא says both readings are 'in the day'. The reading then is still called the 'night' reading only
- **Berakhot 9a, step 1** (terutzHavaAmina): Same reversal as in whatsHappening; the sunrise ברייתא's difficulty is day-wording vs. a night reading. Source: Berakhot 8b: אלמא קודם הנץ החמה יממא הוא... אלמא ליליא הוא.
  - was: The ברייתא calls the time after dawn 'night,' yet treats a reading then as the daytime שמע, which seems contradictory.
  - now: The ברייתא calls the time before sunrise 'day' (both readings are 'in the day'), yet treats a reading then as the nighttime שמע, which seems contradictory.
- **Berakhot 11a, step 18** (phrases.6.notes): The site gives the Rashba's reading of the parable as the plain meaning without attribution; Rashi reads it the opposite way (the man will cut off the praised beard). (The note sits in the phrase's notes field, shown in brackets after the English.) Source: Berakhot 11a (יִהְיֶה כְּנֶגֶד הַמַּשְׁחִיתִים); Rashi s.v. לאחד שאמרו לו זקנך מגודל: הגדול הזה יהיה נתון לתער ולמספרים שאביא עליו ואשחיתנו; Rashi s.v. עכשיו שאני הטתי.
  - was: He means: I grow it only to oppose people who shave. A compliment turned into a jab.
  - now: So the רשב״א: I grow it only to oppose people who shave. Rashi reads it differently: since you praised it, I will hand it over to the destroyers, the razor and scissors. So too, when רבי אלעזר leaned, as if praising רבי ישמעאל's way, רבי ישמעאל changed.
- **Berakhot 11a, step 10** (deeperAnalysis): The site gives a non-Rashi explanation of the groom's distraction as the only one; Rashi says he is preoccupied with the mitzvah of the first union. Source: Berakhot 11a (הַאי טְרִיד וְהַאי לָא טְרִיד); Rashi s.v. הכונס את הבתולה פטור: שטרוד במחשבת בעילת מצוה.
  - was: The man marrying a בתולה is worried that he may find she is not a בתולה, so he cannot focus.
  - now: The man marrying a בתולה is preoccupied with thoughts of the first marital union, the בעילת מצוה (Rashi; others explain he worries he may find she is not a בתולה), so he cannot focus.
- **Berakhot 15b, step 1** (confusionAlert): The alert says both agree hearing is required from the start, but this same amud (step 10, and 15a step 25) says רבי יהודה allows it even לכתחילה, so the alert states as settled what the גמרא goes on to reject. Source: Berakhot 15b: לְעוֹלָם רַבִּי יְהוּדָה הִיא, וַאֲפִילּוּ לְכַתְּחִלָּה נָמֵי; 15a: דְּאִי רַבִּי יְהוּדָה, אֲפִילּוּ לְכַתְּחִלָּה נָמֵי יָצָא.
  - was: Remember that the argument between רבי יוסי and רבי יהודה is only about after the fact. Both agree that in the first place you should hear what you are saying.
  - now: The גמרא has not yet settled what רבי יהודה holds. Here it tests the idea that he allows it only after the fact, but in step 10 it says he allows it even from the start. רבי יוסי says it does not count even after the fact.
- **Berakhot 15b, step 21** (deeperAnalysis): The גמרא names the lenient view on hearing as רבי יהודה's, and Rashi says שניהם means רבי יהודה and רבי יוסי. Source: Berakhot 15a: יָצָא, דִּבְרֵי רַבִּי יְהוּדָה; Rashi 15b s.v. הלכה כדברי שניהם להקל: הלכה כר' יהודה דלא בעי שמיעה והלכה כר' יוסי דלא בעי דקדוק.
  - was: the lenient one is the תנא קמא, who says it counts
  - now: the lenient one is רבי יהודה, who says it counts
- **Berakhot 15b, step 21** (whatToRemember): Same fix: the lenient view on hearing is רבי יהודה's, as the גמרא and Rashi say. Source: Rashi 15b s.v. הלכה כדברי שניהם להקל: הלכה כר' יהודה דלא בעי שמיעה.
  - was: (like the תנא קמא)
  - now: (like רבי יהודה)
- **Berakhot 15b, step 21** (confusionAlert): שניהם refers to the two תנאים (רבי יהודה and רבי יוסי), not to 'two disputes'. Rashi says so explicitly. Source: Rashi 15b s.v. הלכה כדברי שניהם להקל: הלכה כר' יהודה ... והלכה כר' יוסי.
  - was: 'Both of them' does not mean two people agree. It means two separate disputes, and in each one we pick the lenient side.
  - now: 'Both of them' means רבי יהודה and רבי יוסי. They argue in two disputes, and in each one we follow whichever of them is lenient: רבי יהודה on hearing, רבי יוסי on the letters.
- **Bekhorot 51a, step 11** (deeperAnalysis): The גמרא never asks what 'לפיכך' refers to; on 51b it only notes that this line teaches the ברייתא of נטלו והחזירו לו. Source: Bekhorot 51b: לְפִיכָךְ אִם רָצָה הַכֹּהֵן... תְּנֵינָא לְהָא דְּתָנוּ רַבָּנַן... נְטָלוֹ וְהֶחְזִירוֹ לוֹ — יָצָא.
  - was: and the גמרא will later discuss what 'therefore' refers to.
  - now: and the גמרא on the next amud shows that this matches a ברייתא about a כהן who takes the money and gives it back.
- **Bekhorot 51a, step 11** (phrases.5.notes): Same error as in deeperAnalysis: no such question about 'לפיכך' appears in the גמרא. Source: Bekhorot 51b: תְּנֵינָא לְהָא דְּתָנוּ רַבָּנַן... נְטָלוֹ וְהֶחְזִירוֹ לוֹ — יָצָא.
  - was: The גמרא will later ask what 'therefore' is connected to.
  - now: On the next amud the גמרא shows this matches a ברייתא: a כהן who takes the money and gives it back.
- **Bekhorot 53b, step 30** (deeperAnalysis): In the פסוק, 'שנה שנה' is at the end of the verse, not directly after 'עשר תעשר'. Source: Devarim 14:22: עַשֵּׂר תְּעַשֵּׂר אֵת כׇּל תְּבוּאַת זַרְעֶךָ הַיֹּצֵא הַשָּׂדֶה שָׁנָה שָׁנָה.
  - was: come right after "עשר תעשר"
  - now: come at the end of the same פסוק as "עשר תעשר"
- **Bekhorot 56b, step 1** (stepSummary): Per Rashi the challenge is that a fetus given to her is not exempted from tithe as a gift; the animal is still אתנן (if it comes out tenth it is eaten with a blemish), and the next steps depend on it being real אתנן. Source: Bekhorot 56b וְתִיעַשְּׂרֵהּ אִיהִי; Rashi s.v. ותיעשריה איהי: כיון דבמעי אמו נתנו לה לא מיפסיל משום נתן במתנה ויאכל במומו אם יצא האתנן בעשירי.
  - was: if the calf is not אתנן, the זונה should be able to tithe it herself
  - now: if an animal given while still a fetus is not exempt as a gift, the זונה should be able to tithe it herself
- **Bekhorot 56b, step 1** (whatsHappening): Same error: Rashi says the point is that the gift-in-the-womb does not exempt it, not that it was never אתנן. Source: Rashi s.v. ותיעשריה איהי (Bekhorot 56b).
  - was: If the unborn calf was never אתנן, why
  - now: If an animal given while still a fetus is not exempt from tithe as a gift, why
- **Bekhorot 56b, step 1** (deeperAnalysis): Same error: the animal is still אתנן per Rashi (eaten with a blemish if it comes out tenth); only the gift exemption is avoided. Saying it was never אתנן also undercuts step 4, which needs the case to be real אתנן. Source: Rashi s.v. ותיעשריה איהי (Bekhorot 56b): לא מיפסיל משום נתן במתנה ויאכל במומו אם יצא האתנן בעשירי.
  - was: so it never got the status of אתנן. If so, the calf is a normal animal that belongs to the זונה.
  - now: so it is not exempted as an animal she received as a gift. If so, the calf belongs to the זונה and is subject to tithing in her hands.
- **Bekhorot 55a, step 8** (mimraCoreRuling): This is only the inference from רבי אמי's bridge rule (אלמא), which the Gemara then drops with אלא in favor of רבי יוחנן's verse-based reason; stating it as the bare ruling is misleading. Source: Bekhorot 55a אַלְמָא, מִשּׁוּם דְּלָא מְיקָרְבָן הוּא ... אֶלָּא אָמַר רַבִּי חִיָּיא בַּר אַבָּא.
  - was: The reason the יַרְדֵּן divides is that
  - now: According to רבי אמי, the reason the יַרְדֵּן divides is that
- **Arakhin 4a, step 12** (deeperAnalysis): A זר is forbidden to eat תרומה and קדשים, not to touch them; the Gemara's point is only that he cannot eat with the כהן. Source: Arakhin 4a: אִי בָּעֵי זָר לְמֵיכַל בַּהֲדֵי כֹּהֵן לָא מָצֵי אָכֵיל.
  - was: a זר is not allowed to touch
  - now: a זר is not allowed to eat

## 202611060149-rev92-review-fixes

Approved: Drafted by Claude from the Nov 5 2026 review and checked against the Gemara on Sefaria; David asked for them on Nov 5. Awaiting review by a rav.

- **Niddah 40a, step 21** (stepSummary): The summary said all twins need two קרבנות. The Gemara means twins born far apart; a ברייתא here says one קרבן covers births close together. Source: Niddah 40a (the ברייתא on 'זאת תורת היולדת' and the case of יהודה וחזקיה); Niddah 27a.
  - was: ״לבן או לבת״ teaches that even twins from one pregnancy need two.
  - now: ״לבן או לבת״ teaches that even twins from one pregnancy need two when they are born far apart, like רבי חייא's sons (see 27a). Twins born together still need only one.
- **Niddah 40a, step 21** (whatToRemember): Same over-generalisation as the summary. Source: Niddah 40a.
  - was: and ״לבן או לבת״ covers twins from one pregnancy. Each birth needs its own קרבן.
  - now: and ״לבן או לבת״ covers twins from one pregnancy born far apart. Twins born together still need only one קרבן.
- **Niddah 40a, step 15** (deeperAnalysis): Said one קרבן never covers several births, against the ברייתא on this amud. Source: Niddah 40a.
  - was: That means each birth creates its own obligation, so one קרבן does not cover several births.
  - now: So a birth that comes after the earlier birth's days are over needs its own קרבן, even for twins from one pregnancy. Births close together, like ordinary twins, are still covered by one קרבן.
- **Keritot 10b** (overview): The proof that ends at the top of 10b is אביי's, not רב אשי's; the site's own 10a page says so. Source: Keritot 10a, 'אמר אביי תא שמע: שלשים...'.
  - was: The amud opens by finishing רב אשי's proof
  - now: The amud opens by finishing אביי's proof (begun on 10a)
- **Keritot 10b, step 1** (stepSummary): Same wrong attribution. Source: Keritot 10a.
  - was: The גמרא finishes רב אשי's first proof.
  - now: The גמרא finishes אביי's proof, begun on 10a.
- **Keritot 10b, step 2** (title): 'A second' implied the first proof was also רב אשי's. Source: Keritot 10a–10b.
  - was: רב אשי brings a second ברייתא about the six days
  - now: רב אשי brings another ברייתא, about the six days
- **Chullin 110a, step 5** (stepSummary): The Oct 31 repair changed 'In plain words' to give the plain 'udder' to רב חייא, but the summary still gave it to רב יצחק בר אבודימי, so the step told two different stories without saying there are two readings. Source: Chullin 110a, 'ומפלפולו של רב חייא שנה ליה כחל סתם'; Steinsaltz there.
  - was: reports that רב יצחק בר אבודימי did teach about the udder of a nursing animal, but said it as a plain 'udder'.
  - now: reports that רב יצחק בר אבודימי taught about the udder of a nursing animal, and that רב חייא, relying on רב's sharpness, passed it on to רב as a plain 'udder'. (Some, like the Steinsaltz commentary, read it as רב יצחק בר אבודימי himself saying a plain 'udder'.)
- **Chullin 110a** (overview): Matches the repaired step 5. Source: Chullin 110a.
  - was: In a second version, he taught about a nursing animal's udder but said only 'udder'.
  - now: In a second version, he taught about a nursing animal's udder, and רב חייא passed it on to רב as a plain 'udder'.
- **Bekhorot 50a, step 10** (stepSummary): The Oct 31 weekly pass changed the translation to Rashi's reading (paying each year separately) but left the old wording in the summary. Source: Bekhorot 50a, 'קילבון לפרוטרוט', and Rashi there.
  - was: an extra פונדיון is a fee for changing a large coin into small coins.
  - now: the extra פונדיון is an exchange fee (קילבון), because he pays year by year instead of all at once.
- **Bekhorot 50a, step 10** (whatsHappening): Matches the repaired translation. Source: Bekhorot 50a, Rashi.
  - was: The ברייתא explains that the extra פונדיון is a money-changer's fee and not part of the סלע itself.
  - now: The ברייתא explains that the extra פונדיון is an exchange fee for paying year by year, and not part of the סלע itself.
